YES, Anyi. I went through the **8:00 PM package as the real baseline**, compared it against the runtime behavior and the compiler errors you hit afterward, and I found something very important before we touch anything else.

The `SET_VEHICLE_HALT`, `World.IsWaypointActive`, and obsolete `FightAgainst()` errors you got at 9:09 PM are **not actually present in the 8:00 PM source package**. The current 8:00 PM source already uses `Game.IsWaypointActive`, has no `SET_VEHICLE_HALT`, and has no `FightAgainst()`. So the 9:09 build was compiling a **mixed revision** from the previous patch rather than the exact 8:00 PM baseline. The new synchronized package below is deliberately based on the 8:00 PM source and strips those unsupported APIs out entirely.

Your runtime itself also confirms that the foundation is genuinely alive: the real `scripts` path is being used, GangData has 15 gangs / 432 MemberPool hashes / 114 turf zones, Chaos Activity has 18 entries, Chaos audio is resolved, NAudio is present, Police Authority is active, and your PolIgnus/Venti profile is loaded.

The important problem now is **gameplay state**, not whether the whole mod exists.

### What I am fixing in this revision

The biggest one is the suspect lifecycle.

Right now your game can create a dispatch-owned suspect, but ordinary crime types such as suspicious gang activity, arms dealing, kidnapping, etc. can arrive at the scene with the ped doing basically nothing because the old `EnsureSceneSubject()` creates the ped, marks `BlockPermanentEvents = true`, and doesn't necessarily give it an actual behavior until certain event types qualify. That's exactly why you're seeing your "statue suspect." The current Core source confirms that scene subjects are spawned separately and only certain types/severity get an immediate combat/flee task. 

The new behavior is:

`Dispatch accepted → suspect exists → Anyi reaches scene → scene behavior actually starts`

Gang ambush / mass shootout / officer distress / high severity → **resist and fight**

Ped pursuit / kidnapping / hijacking / vehicle pursuit / reckless driver → **flee**

Normal criminal scene → **stay wary, look at Anyi, remain interactable**, not freeze forever.

That means the suspect isn't going to run away before you even arrive, but he also isn't going to stand there like an NPC mannequin after you arrive.

### The surrender bug is being properly fixed

Your runtime repeatedly says:

> `Order the suspect to show hands and reach a compliant state first.`

and then you never get the actual hands-up progression.

The old logic was declaring `SuspectCompliant` too eagerly when the surrender command was issued.

I changed that into a real two-step process:

**First Secure press:**
Police command → suspect receives `HandsUp()` → state remains pending → notification says wait.

**After ~1.5 seconds:**
If the suspect is still alive, not shooting and not fighting → state becomes `SuspectCompliant` → actual hands-up notification.

**Second Secure press:**
Now the suspect is actually arrested/secured.

So the gameplay should finally feel like:

> "Show me your hands."

then

> *suspect actually puts hands up*

then

> "You're under arrest."

rather than the mod pretending a hands-up task happened instantaneously.

The current source already had the concept of `SurrenderRequested`; I tightened the transition so the first press doesn't lie about the suspect already being compliant.

### Death is no longer allowed to magically finish the job from across the city

I kept your `CompleteDispatchOnSuspectDeath=True`, because you explicitly want a resisting suspect that you neutralize to be able to finish the event.

But it now depends on being **at the scene**.

So:

`Suspect dies while Anyi is nearby` → dispatch can close successfully.

`Suspect gets killed by a random gang or another NPC while Anyi is still driving there` → dispatch does **not** magically disappear.

This is especially important because you've had gangs interfering with your scenes and wondered whether *they* killed the suspect.

Your runtime already shows the Police system can detect gang attacks such as Santos Vagos attacking Police Authority Anyi, and the dispatch system correctly reaches `Offered → Accepted → EnRoute`.

### The duplicate Venti / duplicate PolIgnus problem is fixed at the architecture level

This one is BIG. 😂

Your current runtime showed response units being created with the same model hashes as your personal player profile. The previous response code used the current profile's vehicle/model for response units, which meant your Police world could become:

> Anyi Venti + another Venti + another PolIgnus.

That's why your screenshots looked like a Venti convention. 😭

The new profile structure separates:

`OfficerModel` = **your Anyi Police character**

`VehicleModel` = **your personal patrol vehicle**

from:

`ResponseOfficerModel` = **spawned responding officer**

`ResponseVehicleModel` = **spawned response car**

and the convoy driver uses the response officer model too.

For your PolIgnus profile, the intended configuration is now effectively:

```text
Player Officer = your configured model
Player Vehicle = polignus

Response Officer = s_m_y_cop_01
Response Vehicle = police

Transport Officer = s_m_y_cop_01
Transport Vehicle = stockade
```

So PolIgnus stays **your car** instead of every responding unit stealing your favorite car.

The current response source now explicitly reads `ResponseVehicleModel` and `ResponseOfficerModel` rather than blindly using the player profile. 

### The convoy is finally being treated as a real custody operation

Your old runtime showed the convoy getting into a horrible repeating condition where the prisoner entity was lost and the watchdog just kept reporting the same error over and over.

That's not acceptable.

The new convoy now has a real lifecycle:

`Arrested`

→ `PickupEnRoute`

→ `Escorting`

→ `HoldingAtStation`

→ `PrisonTransfer`

→ `Completed`

and importantly, it no longer automatically sends the prisoner to prison eight seconds after reaching the station.

Instead:

**Prisoner arrives at police station**

You get:

> **PRISONER ARRIVED**
> Prisoner is securely held.
> Press your **Transport** key again to approve prison transfer, or **Reject** to close the justice task.

That matches your idea almost exactly.

Your existing `R` transport shortcut remains untouched.

So:

`R` at Arrested → call prisoner transport

`R` again while HoldingAtStation → **approve prison transfer**

`N` / your configured Reject shortcut while HoldingAtStation → decline the prison transfer and close the justice operation cleanly.

That means you don't need another arbitrary new key.

The current convoy code now contains `ContinueToPrison()` and `DeclineAtStation()` specifically for that behavior. 

### Convoy failure is now self-cleaning

If:

* transport is destroyed,
* driver dies,
* prisoner disappears,
* prisoner dies unexpectedly,

the convoy transitions to `Compromised`, cleans its own entities/state, and tells Dispatch that the custody operation failed.

It does **not** sit there for another five minutes printing:

> prisoner entity lost
> convoy compromised
> prisoner entity lost
> convoy compromised

😂

The current revised `CheckCustody()` explicitly calls cleanup after those failures.

### Arrest no longer silently completes the dispatch

This is also important.

Your intended behavior is:

`Arrested` ≠ `Completed`

Instead:

`Arrested → AwaitingTransport → convoy → prison → booking → Completed`

The current Dispatch source already records:

> `Arrest phase complete; dispatch remains active until custody/transport finishes.` 

That's exactly the direction we're keeping.

### And I fixed the response-unit cleanup

The current `CompleteSuccessful()` now explicitly releases the assigned response unit before clearing the reference. 

That matters because your heartbeats were still showing:

`ResponseUnits=1`

after some dispatches had already finished.

Now a completed or failed call can release its Police-owned response unit instead of leaving the unit hanging around indefinitely.

### The Gang data itself does NOT need to be rebuilt

This is one area I absolutely do **not** want to break.

Your diagnostic proves:

```text
GangData = 15 gangs
MemberPool = 432 hashes
TurfZoneData = 114 zones
PlayerGang = Anyiii's Gang
```

So the external data integration is healthy. 

The subtle issue is that `MemberPool.xml` is a **global model pool**; it does not necessarily give us the exact gang identity for every hash by itself.

So the new Police connector treats it correctly:

```text
GangData match
    → exact gang identity

MemberPool-only match
    → known Gang Mod member model,
      but gang identity unresolved
```

We don't invent "this is Ballas" just because a hash happens to be in MemberPool.

That's much safer and much more faithful to your actual Gang & Turf data.

### Your traffic / NPC interaction is already partly alive

Your logs actually prove this subsystem isn't dead.

You have:

`POLICE_TRAFFIC_STOP | Traffic stop initiated`

followed by:

`Traffic offender interview | Documents requested/presented`

and you also have civilian paper/report interactions.

So the interaction layer isn't starting from zero.

The problem is that it's still too restrictive and sometimes repeatedly interacts with the same target.

The current PED interaction file confirms it deliberately filters out gang members and only selects a relatively safe pedestrian candidate. 

The hotfix keeps the system stateful and broadens the context while preserving the safety boundary. The traffic-stop logic remains event-driven rather than hammering vehicle tasks every frame.

### Your G-key grenade problem is also handled

This one is hilarious but absolutely valid. 😂

Your current configured NPC interaction key is `G`. The diagnostic explicitly confirms that. 

The Police hotkey handler now suppresses GTA's native control 58 in both relevant control groups while your configured G interaction shortcut is physically held.

So your Police interaction should no longer also perform the normal GTA grenade/throw action.

And I am **not changing your existing shortcut assignments**.

Your current:

```text

E Secure

G NPC

```

remains authoritative.

### The LemonUI text-merging problem is being cleaned up

You're right that the Controls menu was trying to show too much text.

Instead of giant combined rows like:

> ACCEPT Y / REJECT N + several lines of description

the updated Main UI uses compact control rows:

```text
Accept: Y
Reject: N
Secure: E
Transport: R
NPC: G
Investigate: I
```

Your current Dispatch already has the important pieces: Offered → Accepted → EnRoute → OnScene → Investigating → fleeing/resisting/compliant → Arrested, and it already supports death completing the call when configured.

Anyii at 10:00PM :

Today 10:00PM, i already compiled your new current packages about the convoy and dispatch thingy. Just now, i got this error while about to compile to dll. What is this and what happening in this? GPT Ayaka : Hehe nooo 😂 — **your GTA root is NOT being moved to AppData.** The error is actually just a bad C# condition I accidentally introduced in `AnyiLSPDChaosAudio.cs`.

The screenshot shows the problematic line around **line 420**:

```csharp
if (!string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(root) &&
    !root.IndexOf("\\AppData\\Local\\assembly\\", StringComparison.OrdinalIgnoreCase) < 0)
```

The problem is the second `!`.

`string.IndexOf(...)` returns an `int`, usually:

```text
-1 = not found
0 or greater = found
```

So this:

```csharp
!root.IndexOf(...)
```

is illegal because C# cannot apply `!` to an integer. That's exactly your:

```text
CS0023: Operator '!' cannot be applied to operand of type 'int'
```

### Fix exactly this line

In:

```text
AnyiLSPDChaosAudio.cs
```

around **line 420**, replace the whole condition with:

```csharp
if (!string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(root) &&
    root.IndexOf("\\AppData\\Local\\assembly\\", StringComparison.OrdinalIgnoreCase) >= 0)
```

So the complete block becomes:

```csharp
try
{
    string root =
        Path.GetDirectoryName(_config.ChaosAudioRoot);

    if (!string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(root) &&
        root.IndexOf("\\AppData\\Local\\assembly\\", StringComparison.OrdinalIgnoreCase) >= 0)
    {
        return Path.Combine(
            root,
            AnyiLSPDPoliceConfig.FileName);
    }
}
catch { }
```

However — and this is the important part — **I don't actually want this block to treat AppData as your normal root at all.** Your real deployment root is still:

```text
C:\Users\Nataniel\STEAM\steamapps\common\Grand Theft Auto V Enhanced\scripts
```

The AppData path you saw earlier came from the .NET assembly loader's shadow/cached assembly location. That is a runtime loading location, **not your GTA scripts directory**.

So I'd actually make this cleaner and safer.

Replace that entire AppData-detection block with:

```csharp
private string GetPoliceIniPath()
{
    try
    {
        string scriptDirectory = AnyiLSPDPathProvider.ScriptDirectory;

        if (!string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(scriptDirectory))
        {
            return Path.Combine(
                scriptDirectory,
                AnyiLSPDPoliceConfig.FileName);
        }
    }
    catch { }

    return null;
}
```

That is the version I prefer for your project because it follows the architecture we established: **the Police INI belongs beside the DLL in the actual GTA `scripts` directory.**

So:

```text
GTA V Enhanced
└── scripts
    ├── AnyiLSPDImmersiveResponse.dll
    ├── AnyiLSPDPolice.ini
    ├── ChaosResponse.Audio
    ├── NAudio.dll
    └── ...
```

Not:

```text
AppData\Local\assembly\...
```

and absolutely not:

```text
scripts\scripts
```

### Why did AppData appear at all?

This is the funny part. 😂

.NET/SHVDN can load assemblies through an internal assembly-loading/cache location. That's why you previously discovered something like:

```text
C:\Users\Carl Gap\AppData\Local\assembly\dl3\...
```

That doesn't mean your mod decided:

> “Hmm yes, let's move Anyi's GTA installation to AppData.”

🤣

It's basically a **runtime assembly-loading/cache location**.

The mistake in my new code was that I tried to detect that situation from the audio path, and I wrote the `IndexOf()` condition incorrectly.

Even more importantly, **the audio system shouldn't use that runtime cache path as the authoritative configuration location in the first place.** Your scripts directory should remain authoritative.

```text
CS0023
Operator '!' cannot be applied to operand of type 'int'
```

is completely gone.

is unrelated to this error. It's just telling us that field is currently assigned but not read. It isn't what's preventing the DLL from compiling.

gpt ayaka :

Yes — and I want to be completely straight with you, Anyi. 🥴

**No, I cannot honestly say the AppData-related logic exists only in `AnyiLSPDChaosAudio.cs`.** The files you gave me show that the project has had **multiple generations of path/logging code**, and some of the older ones really did use the SHVDN shadow-copy location as a runtime path.

The good news is that the **newer `AnyiLSPDPathProvider_v4.cs` is specifically designed to prevent that**. It explicitly says it does not use `Assembly.Location` as the primary path because SHVDN can shadow-copy managed DLLs into `AppData\Local\assembly\dl3`; instead it resolves the real GTA root from the game process/current directory and then constructs the real `scripts` path. 

And we have hard evidence that this newer resolver worked. Your later runtime log reported:

`GameRoot=C:\Users\Nataniel\STEAM\steamapps\common\Grand Theft Auto V Enhanced`

and

`Scripts=C:\Users\Nataniel\STEAM\steamapps\common\Grand Theft Auto V Enhanced\scripts`

with `ShadowCopyScripts=False`. It also correctly resolved your real `gangModData`, `ChaosResponse.GangActivity`, `ChaosResponse.Audio`, and `NAudio.dll` paths. 

So that part is good.

But there are **three separate things** here, and this is where the confusion came from.

The first is **intentional AppData detection**. `AnyiLSPDPathProvider_v4.cs` contains `IsShadowCopyPath()`. It looks for `AppData\Local\assembly\dl3` specifically so the code can recognize a shadow-copy path and reject it as the real GTA root. That's a detection mechanism, not a destination. 

The second is **old code that actually used the shadow-copy directory**. Your older runtime log proves that an earlier build really was writing logs, configs and GangData paths under AppData. For example, that older runtime reported `ScriptDirectory=C:\Users\Carl Gap\AppData\Local\assembly\dl3\...`, and then constructed `GangRoot` and `ChaosResponse.Audio` from that same location.  

That was a genuine bug in the older build. So you were absolutely right to be suspicious when you found those logs.

The third is the **specific new mistake I introduced in `AnyiLSPDChaosAudio(7).cs`**. That file first correctly asks `AnyiLSPDPathProvider.ScriptsDirectory` for the real scripts directory, but then I added a second fallback which tries to derive another path from `_config.ChaosAudioRoot`. Worse, the condition itself is malformed:

```csharp
!root.IndexOf("\\AppData\\Local\\assembly\\", StringComparison.OrdinalIgnoreCase) < 0
```

That's exactly what caused your compiler error. And yes, that block is unnecessarily suspicious and should not be there at all. 

So I don't want to bullshit you with “it's just a harmless fallback.” **That fallback was my bad.** It should be removed.

There is another important piece: your `LSPDMainUI.cs` also had an **older embedded logger implementation** that considered `Assembly.GetExecutingAssembly().Location`, `AppDomain.CurrentDomain.BaseDirectory`, and other candidate directories. That older implementation is precisely the kind of code that can accidentally select the SHVDN shadow-copy directory. 

Meanwhile, the newer `LSPDMainUI.cs` version you showed in another upload already uses:

```csharp
string selected = AnyiLSPDPathProvider.ScriptsDirectory;
```

and writes the logger there. 

So we have **old and new revisions mixed across the project**. That's the real source of the mess.

And that's why I don't want you thinking:

> “Ayaka secretly moved my entire mod to AppData.”

No. 😭

The actual situation is:

**SHVDN itself can shadow-copy the DLL into AppData.**
**Some of our older logger/path code accidentally followed that shadow-copy location.**
**The newer PathProvider was written specifically to stop doing that.**
**The newest ChaosAudio file I gave you contains one unnecessary AppData fallback that should now be removed.**

Your latest correct path architecture should be brutally simple:

```text
GTA V Enhanced
│
├── gangModData\
│   ├── GangData.xml
│   ├── MemberPool.xml
│   ├── TurfZoneData.xml
│   └── ...
│
└── scripts\
    ├── AnyiLSPDImmersiveResponse.dll
    ├── AnyiLSPDPolice.ini
    ├── AnyiLSPD_Runtime.log
    ├── AnyiLSPD_Heartbeat.log
    ├── AnyiLSPD_PoliceAuthority_Diagnostic.log
    ├── NAudio.dll
    ├── ChaosResponse.Audio\
    ├── ChaosResponse.GangActivity\
    └── ...
```

And **nothing in the LSPD code should choose AppData as a working/config/log/data directory.**

One subtle exception is that the log may still print something like:

`Assembly=C:\Users\Carl Gap\AppData\Local\assembly\dl3\...`

and that is okay **as a diagnostic description of where SHVDN loaded the DLL from**. Your good runtime log already demonstrates this: the `Assembly=` field points into AppData while `ScriptDirectory=` correctly points to the real GTA `scripts` folder. 

That's actually useful. It tells us:

**Assembly location:** SHVDN shadow-copy/cache
**Working script/data/log location:** real GTA scripts directory

Those are two different things.

So my recommendation now is very firm: **remove every AppData fallback from the actual operational path logic**, not just fix the `!IndexOf` syntax. Keep `IsShadowCopyPath()` in `PathProvider` because that's useful protection, but don't use AppData as a fallback destination.

And yes, you were right to question this before compiling again. 😭 The `ChaosAudio` file I supplied should not have contained that second fallback in the first place.

### About the funny blue box 😂

I checked the actual current package instead of guessing.

For your **player PolIgnus**, `AnyiLSPDCore.cs` already specifically disables emergency lights when the selected vehicle is `polignus`:

```csharp
_emergencyLightsOn = _profiles.Current.EmergencyLights &&
    !string.Equals(_profiles.Current.VehicleModel, "polignus", StringComparison.OrdinalIgnoreCase);
```

That is **AnyiLSPDCore.cs, around line 483–484**.

Then `ApplyEmergencyState()` is around **lines 915–920**:

```csharp
Function.Call(Hash.SET_VEHICLE_LIGHTS, vehicle, _emergencyLightsOn ? 2 : 0);
Function.Call(Hash.SET_VEHICLE_SIREN, vehicle, _emergencySirenOn && sirenAllowed);
```

And your XML profile explicitly has:

```xml
<EmergencyLights>false</EmergencyLights>
<NativeSiren>false</NativeSiren>
```

for PolIgnus. So the normal PolIgnus player patrol is **already protected from the native siren path**.

That means I would **not change ChaosAudio** for that blue rectangle. The more likely culprit is the **PolIgnus vehicle asset/emergency-light visual itself**, or an emergency-light action being applied to another spawned police vehicle. Your convoy code separately handles emergency lights for the transport vehicle and cleans them up afterward. So before touching anything, I'd test whether that blue box appears specifically when using **Emergency Signals** or only when a spawned police/transport unit arrives.

The exact files to investigate are therefore:

`AnyiLSPDCore.cs` → `ApplyEmergencyState()` / `ToggleEmergency()`

and

`AnyiLSPDConvoy.cs` → the transport vehicle emergency-light handling.

Not `AnyiLSPDChaosAudio.cs`. 👍

No prisoner transport unless a particular gang member actually becomes an arrestable suspect.
